# Implementation questions and provisional decisions

Gaps found in the spec (revision 549i; revision 549 until 2026-10-12, then 549a, 549b and 549c until 2026-10-15, then 549d and 549e until 2026-10-16, then 549f, 549g and 549h until 2026-10-19) during implementation. Every row is provisional until the owner confirms it; confirmed rows return to the spec as new D-n decisions in a later export. Code that relies on a row carries a `# Q-n` comment.

Status is one of: `provisional` (a new row), `confirmed` (the owner accepted the provisional decision), `changed (#n)` (the owner chose otherwise; the row's decision is updated and the code follows in issue #n: the row's own ticket when the owner answered during its build, otherwise an issue implemented on its own), or `in spec (D-n, revision r)` (returned to the spec).

| ID | Spec location | Gap | Provisional decision | Rationale | Alternatives | Detecting test | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Q-1 | §2.3 | No minimum Python version is stated (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `requires-python >= 3.11`; development and CI use Python 3.14 (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | owner: 3.10 is past the end of its security support, and the project works on 3.14 | ≥3.9 (gsplat allows ≥3.7); ≥3.10 (the first provisional decision, from fused-local-corr's cp310–cp313 wheels) | install on 3.11 in the M18 CI matrix | changed (#1) |
| Q-2 | §2.3, `[fast]` row | The §2.3 table lists fused-ssim in `[fast]`, but fused-ssim is not on PyPI, and a git-URL dependency would block a PyPI upload (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `[fast]` stays an empty extra; the README says to install fused-ssim from its MIT repository; M6, which first uses it, imports it optionally and names the commit it was tested with | keeps the M18 PyPI release possible; owner, 2026-10-11: the real fix is fused-ssim publishing to PyPI, and until then `[fast]` stays empty with a manual install in the README, as Q-6 does for fused-local-corr on Python 3.14 | `fused-ssim @ git+…@<commit>` in `[fast]`; drop `[fast]` | M18 upload dry run; M6 optional-import test | confirmed |
| Q-3 | §2.3, torch row ("≥2.1, CUDA build") | Package metadata can only name a version range: it can't pick a CUDA version other than the one PyPI's wheel bundles, require CUDA on Windows, or stop a user from installing a CPU build (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `torch>=2.1` in metadata. On Linux x86_64 this resolves to PyPI's default torch, which is a CUDA build (2.14.1 depends on the CUDA 13.0 libraries on Linux; source log #4). The README says so and points to the PyTorch index for other CUDA versions and for Windows. CI installs the CPU wheel on purpose, because its runners have no GPU. A test fails when a GPU is present but torch is a CPU build (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | owner: training needs a CUDA build for performance, and CUDA wheels are available; on Linux the default already is one | per-CUDA extras through indexes (not expressible in metadata); requiring a `+cuXXX` local version (not hosted on PyPI) | `tests/test_environment.py`: on a machine where `nvidia-smi` lists a GPU, `torch.version.cuda` is set and `torch.cuda.is_available()` is true | changed (#1) |
| Q-4 | §2.3 notes, CI scan | §2.3 says CI scans "the resolved environment" and that LGPL needs manual sign-off, but doesn't say which environment CI resolves, how a sign-off is recorded, or how unknown, unrecognised, proprietary and non-commercial licenses and multi-term license expressions are treated (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | Each license string is split into terms on `AND` and `;`; an `OR` choice takes its most favourable option; a distribution takes its worst term; "X or later" is one license, and an exception (`WITH`) is stripped so the base license is judged. **Fail**, no sign-off possible: GPL, AGPL, non-commercial, and a non-MIT albumentations. **Needs sign-off**: LGPL, proprietary, UNKNOWN, unrecognised; the scan fails unless an entry of the committed `scripts/license_signoff.json` matches the name, version and reported license string; entries matching nothing are reported as stale. **Pass**: a fixed list of permissive licenses and MPL-2.0 only. CI scans `.[dev,init]` plus the harness on CPU torch; the CUDA torch environment, with its NVIDIA runtime wheels, is scanned locally and at M18 (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | §2.3 requires every dependency to be Apache-compatible; a sign-off that CI doesn't enforce is no sign-off; proprietary NVIDIA runtime wheels (17 in the CUDA 13.0 torch environment) are installed by the user and never redistributed, so they need a recorded decision rather than an impossible failure; owner: a non-commercial license fails outright | fail on everything not permissive; non-commercial to sign-off like proprietary; scan only `.[dev]` in CI | `tests/test_license_scan.py` | changed (#1) |
| Q-5 | §2.1, `ftgs_harness` row; §3.3 | §2.1 says "separate package", which could mean a separate import package or a separate distribution (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | A distribution `ftgs-harness` with the import package `ftgs_harness`, in `harness/`, with its own pyproject and its own copy of the Apache-2.0 LICENSE, and no dependency on openftgs; its `ftgs-harness` console script arrives with the commands at M16. matplotlib, the owner's decision for the harness plots (`docs/clean-room/milestones.md`, M16, 2026-10-11), is declared now rather than at M16; §2.3 doesn't list it, so the CI license scan checks it (matplotlib 3.11.2 reports the PSF license; its dependencies are BSD, MIT or Apache) (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | §3.3 names the commands `ftgs-harness run` and `ftgs-harness rerun` (spec lines 1277–1278) and says the harness imports neither implementation; a separate distribution matches both and keeps matplotlib out of openftgs installs; declaring matplotlib now puts it under the license scan from the start | subpackage inside the openftgs wheel; declare matplotlib at M16 | `tests/test_skeleton.py`: the harness pyproject names `ftgs-harness` and no openftgs dependency, and the root pyproject packages only `src/`; CI's wheel job: the openftgs wheel has no `ftgs_harness/` files and the harness wheel no `openftgs/` files | confirmed |
| Q-6 | §2.3, fused-local-corr row and the RoMa note | fused-local-corr publishes wheels only for CPython 3.10–3.13 and no sdist, so `[init]` can't resolve on Python 3.14, the version the project uses (Q-1) (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `[init]` lists `fused-local-corr>=0.2.2; sys_platform == 'linux' and python_version < '3.14'`; on 3.14 the README says to build it from its repository at commit 689f2f7 with `pip install --no-build-isolation` (needs nvcc and a CUDA build of torch) (owner decision, 2026-10-11) Since revision 549h, `[init]` lists fused-local-corr on no Python (D-85, from Q-145) | §2.3 calls it an optional speed-up; RoMa matches without it (use_custom_corr off); a source build at 689f2f7 compiled on Python 3.14.8 with torch 2.14.1+cu130 and CUDA 13.0 and passed its own correctness tests on CPU and CUDA (source log #3) | drop it from `[init]` and document it; keep it unconditional, so `[init]` fails on 3.14 until cp314 wheels exist | `pip install .[init]` on 3.14 in CI (M18); U-38 and B-8 with and without it (M12) | in spec (D-85, revision 549h) |
| Q-7 | §3 introduction; §3.1 converter checks ("run when the dataset is available; not part of CI"); §2.3 note on pretrained weights | The spec gates dataset tests and tests that download pretrained weights (RoMa, DINOv2, LPIPS backbones) but names no way to tell the test suite where datasets are or that downloads are allowed (from #1: M0 · Repository skeleton) | `dataset` tests run only when the environment variable `OPENFTGS_DATASETS` names a directory holding the converted datasets; `weights` tests run only when `OPENFTGS_ALLOW_DOWNLOADS=1`; otherwise both are skipped with a reason naming the variable (`tests/conftest.py`) | environment variables work the same locally and in CI, need no pytest options, and keep downloads opt-in so CI never fetches weights | pytest command-line options (`--datasets DIR`, `--allow-downloads`); a config file; probing the default cache directories | `tests/test_markers.py`: tests are skipped without the variables and run with them | confirmed |
| Q-8 | E14, D-46; §2.1 rows for `types` and `motion` | No module or signature computes Δt_f; `data` (Foundation) needs it but may not import `motion` (Core); the even-count median convention and duplicate timestamps within one camera are unstated (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Public `openftgs.types.frame_interval(camera_timestamps: Mapping[str, Sequence[float]], time: TimeNormalizer) -> float`. The median of an even count is the mean of the two middle values; medians are taken over the gaps between each camera's distinct raw timestamps, then divided by T_τ once; a camera counts only with at least two distinct timestamps, and the pooled fallback also uses distinct timestamps, so duplicated timestamps neither shrink Δt_f nor block the fallback; 1.0 for a single timestamp; `ValueError` for an empty mapping or a result that is not finite and > 0. `motion`'s share of E14 is the unit conversions of primitive quantities (μt, s = d/T_τ, v = v_τ·T_τ) (owner answer, 2026-10-11; distinct timestamps per camera after the M1 milestone review, F-2, owner answer 2026-10-11) | `types` is the only module every user of Δt_f (`data`, `init`, `model`, `optim`, `train`) may import; the statistical median is the standard definition; dividing once is exact on dyadic data and identical in real arithmetic; keeping duplicates would let a repeated timestamp shrink Δt_f, against D-46's aim | `data` computes it privately (U-18 then tests `data`); `torch.median`'s lower middle value; keeping duplicates within each camera (E14 read literally; the first confirmed decision) | U-18 (even-count, unsynchronized and duplicated-timestamp cases); S-8 (M11) | changed (#10) |
| Q-9 | §1.4 "Time"; §2.1 `TimeNormalizer` | The warning's category, which conversion warns, the case T_τ = 0, input validation and the dtype of tensor results are unstated (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | `to_t` alone emits a `UserWarning` for inputs not inside the closed interval [τ₀, τ₁], NaN included; T_τ = 0 gives t ≡ 0 and τ ≡ τ₀; `ValueError` for bounds that are not finite or τ₁ < τ₀; floating tensors keep their dtype, integer tensors become float64 (D-30), floats and ints return a float. The tensor range check syncs with the host, which is acceptable because the training step passes Python floats (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | Renders call `to_t`, so the render-time warning has one source; `to_tau` maps primitive time centers, which legitimately leave [0, 1] | A custom warning class (one more public name); warning in `to_tau` too; `RuntimeWarning` | U-18 warning case; U-4 (M4) raises no unexpected warning inside the range | confirmed |
| Q-10 | §2.2 checkpoint manifest ("full TrainConfig"); §2.1 `openftgs.config`; §3.3 `harness_config.json` | No function or layout is given for TrainConfig's JSON form; unknown and missing keys are unspecified (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Public `openftgs.config.config_to_dict(cfg: TrainConfig) -> dict[str, Any]` and `config_from_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> TrainConfig`: nested by sub-config (`{"growth": {"n_max": …}}`), tuples written as lists and `None` as null, missing keys take their defaults, an unknown key is a `ValueError` naming its dotted path, ints accepted for float fields, bools rejected for numeric fields, non-finite or overflowing numbers rejected. The same type checks and normalization (ints to floats, lists to tuples) run in every config class's constructor, so `TrainConfig(...)` and `config_from_dict` accept exactly the same values (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | The nesting matches the harness's dotted names; a silently ignored typo would train a different configuration; partial files keep `--config` short | Methods on `TrainConfig`; a flat dotted-key layout; ignoring unknown keys as §2.2 does for file formats | TrainConfig JSON round trip (`tests/test_config.py`); U-21 checkpoint round trip (M10); M16 harness config | confirmed |
| Q-11 | §2.1 `openftgs.config`; §0 notation domains | Only `sh_degree` has a stated range (D-3); the other fields' ranges are implied by their meaning (from #10: M1 · Types and configuration) | Rejected with `ValueError`: `init_opacity`, `dead_opacity`, `prune_opacity` outside (0, 1); `grad_quantile` outside (0, 1]; learning rates, `eps`, `scale_floor`, `scene_scale_factor`, `split_scale`, `grad_threshold`, `ssim_sigma` ≤ 0; loss weights λ_img, λ_ssim, λ_perc, λ_reg and score weights λ_g, λ_o < 0 (0 allowed: Table 5 ablates λ_reg = 0); `betas` outside [0, 1); counts `iterations`, `every`, `sh_interval`, `points_per_frame`, `scale_knn`, `n_max`, `images_per_step`, `log_every` below 1; `ssim_window` below 1 or even; `prefetch` < 0; `start > stop` in growth and relocation; `reg_until` < 0; `score_rate` outside (0, 1]; background components outside [0, 1] (owner answer, 2026-10-11; score weights named after the M1 milestone review, F-1, owner answer 2026-10-11) | The notation gives σ ∈ (0, 1) and positive rates; invalid values would otherwise fail deep inside training with NaN or empty windows | Validate only `sh_degree` and the `Literal` fields; validate at `Trainer` construction instead of in `__post_init__` | Range cases in `tests/test_config.py`; U-21's shortened test calendar (M10) must still validate | confirmed |
| Q-12 | §2.1 `openftgs.motion` row; §2.4 "Kernels"; D-50 | The spec names motion's functions and `temporal_slice` but gives no signatures and no return type (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `__all__` is exactly: `moved_means(means, velocities, times, t)` (E1); `temporal_opacity(times, log_durations, t)` (E4); `effective_opacity(opacity_logits, times, log_durations, t)` (E3); `reg4d_terms(opacity_logits, times, log_durations, t)` (σ·sg[σt] per row, E8); a frozen dataclass `TemporalSlice` with `means_t` [N, 3], `opacities_t` [N], `quats_n` [N, 4], `scales` [N, 3], `opacities` [N] (σ), `reg_terms` [N] and `passes_cull` [N] (bool, σ_eff ≥ 1/255; named after the glossary's "Culling", since "Visible primitive" means positive screen radii, E6; renamed from `visible` after the two-axis review); `temporal_slice(means, times, log_durations, velocities, log_scales, quats, opacity_logits, t, *, impl="auto") -> TemporalSlice`. t is `float \| Tensor` everywhere; stored tensors are passed positionally; the SH concat and `GaussiansAtTime` are left to M3's `at_time`. Since M3, `__all__` also holds the four E14 conversions of Q-27 (`durations_to_normalized`, `durations_to_raw`, `velocities_to_normalized`, `velocities_to_raw`), and `GaussiansAtTime` is defined in `openftgs.model` as the `TemporalSlice` fields plus `sh` (Q-20); `motion`'s `t: float \| Tensor` doesn't conflict with §2.1's `at_time(t: float)`, which passes a float (owner answer, 2026-10-12, after M3, `at_time`'s first use of the slice) | positional tensors keep the compiled function's guards on dtype, device and rank; returning the mask avoids data-dependent shapes; SH is time-independent and not per-row elementwise work | motion defines and returns `GaussiansAtTime`; a NamedTuple; passing a model object (a band violation) | U-1, U-3, U-28; M3's `at_time` | confirmed |
| Q-13 | §2.4 "Kernels" and "Settings" (`PerfConfig.slice_impl = "auto"`); D-50 | What `"auto"` resolves to, what triggers the eager fallback, and what `"cuda"` does before M15 (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `"auto"` is the compiled implementation on CUDA tensors and the eager one on CPU; under `"auto"` only, a failed compilation falls back to eager with a one-time `UserWarning`; a failed compilation is a dynamo or backend error (`TorchDynamoException`) or dynamo reaching its recompile limit for the slice: the slice is compiled with `fullgraph=True`, so reaching the limit raises `torch._dynamo.exc.FailOnRecompileLimitHit` (a plain `Exception`, caught explicitly) and a graph break raises at compile time; the limit stays at dynamo's default, and U-28 and B-3 count recompilations by dynamo's count of unique graphs, since the `fullgraph` path doesn't count frames; the fallback is sticky: every later `"auto"` call in the process runs eager, for every model and device; an explicit `"compiled"` raises on failure; `"cuda"` raises `NotImplementedError` naming D-50, B-1 and M15; any other value raises `ValueError` (owner answer, 2026-10-11: keep eager on CPU; amended by the owner on 2026-10-12, after the triage of #45: reaching the recompile limit counts as a failed compilation) | the reference backend, which runs on CPU, stays plain PyTorch (§2.4 rule 4), and CPU runs and tests skip compile time; rule 4 itself speaks of the reference backend, not of CPU tensors, so the two coincide only while gsplat needs CUDA; an explicit choice should fail loudly. Reaching the recompile limit otherwise made the slice eager with only a log line, no exception and no Python warning, so an explicit `"compiled"` silently became eager and B-1 could time the eager slice; making dynamo raise is the only supported way to detect it, `fullgraph=True` is public, local to the slice and free per call, and the eager fallback is exact (§2.4 rule 2), so a default `"auto"` run keeps going (source log #36) | `"auto"` compiles everywhere; a silent fallback; at the recompile limit, keeping the silent fallback, warning under both values without raising, the process-global `fail_on_recompile_limit_hit` flag (or patching it around every call), reading dynamo's private counters, or raising the limit | U-28; B-3 (M15) sets D-50's final choice; with dynamo's recompile limit set to 1, alternating float32 and float64 inputs makes `"compiled"` raise on the second call (CPU and CUDA), while on CUDA `"auto"` warns once and then matches eager without further warnings (#51) | changed (#51) |
| Q-14 | §2.1 `openftgs.sh` row; E2; §1.1 (C₀, sh0 initialization) | No signatures, and no statement whether the basis normalizes its directions (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | `__all__` is exactly: `C0: float = 0.28209479177387814`; `sh_basis(dirs, degree) -> Tensor`, [..., 3] → [..., (degree+1)²] in gsplat 1.5.3's band order and signs (real SH keeping the Condon–Shortley phase; l = 1 is C₁·(−y, z, −x)), normalizing its directions itself, `ValueError` for a degree outside {0, 1, 2, 3} and for a bool or non-int degree; `rgb_to_sh0(rgb)` = (rgb − 0.5)/C₀; `sh0_to_rgb(sh0)` = C₀·sh0 + 0.5 (owner answer, 2026-10-11: confirmed; E2's full color stays a private helper of the render backend, with no public color function in `sh`) | E2 defines d as a unit vector; gsplat normalizes its directions too | require unit input | U-6; the gsplat basis test (GPU) | confirmed |
| Q-15 | §3.1 U-28 pass criterion | "≤ 1e-6 relative" is undefined where σt underflows to exactly 0, and the bool mask can flip at the 1/255 boundary under reordered float32 arithmetic (from #15: M2 · Motion, temporal opacity and spherical harmonics) | Per output tensor, ‖a − b‖₂ ≤ rtol·‖b‖₂ (relative L2, the measure U-12 names for gradients); `passes_cull` equal except on rows whose eager σ_eff lies within 1e-6 relative of 1/255 (owner answer, 2026-10-11: confirmed) | elementwise relative error is undefined at exact zeros; with N = 10⁶ random rows a boundary flip has a nonzero probability; measured on torch 2.14.1 (RTX 4090 and CPU, N from 1 to 10⁶): worst relative L2 3.6e-8 for values and 1.6e-7 for gradients, while an elementwise criterion with a floor of 1e-6 times the tensor's largest value fails without any bug (2.0e-3 on `means_t`, 1.4e-2 on the `quats` gradient), because fused float32 kernels round differently where results nearly cancel; the small sizes in U-28 (N = 1 to 2,300) keep a few-row error from being averaged away | elementwise rtol with an absolute floor | U-28 | confirmed |

| Q-19 | E14; §2.2 `ftgs-if` (`duration` = s·T_τ, `velocity` = v/T_τ); §1.3 step 7 ("1 for a single timestamp") | With a single timestamp (T_τ = 0) the export gives a zero duration, which the validator rejects, and divides velocities by zero; the import divides by zero too (from #20: M3 · Model and file I/O) | When T_τ = 0, durations and velocities convert with a time unit of 1 instead of T_τ (duration = s, velocity = v), in both directions of `ftgs-if` and in `from_init_points`; positions and time centers are unaffected (t ≡ 0, Q-9) (owner answer, 2026-10-11) | owner: a single-frame model round-trips and validates. It fills a gap rather than departing from the spec: §2.2's formulas are undefined or invalid at T_τ = 0, and the spec already substitutes 1 there (§1.4 "Δt_f := 1", E14, §1.3 step 7), so a unit of 1 is converting with Δt_f; since Q-30 makes single-frame velocities 0, only the duration half has effect (second opinion after M3, 2026-10-12) | refuse to export a single-frame model | single-frame `ftgs-if` round trip; U-19 with one timestamp | in spec (D-70, revision 549a) |
| Q-20 | §2.1 `openftgs.model` (`at_time` returns `GaussiansAtTime`, which is never defined; `physical()` names no keys); E5; U-5; Q-12 | No definition of `GaussiansAtTime`, no keys for `physical()`, and no public function for E5, which U-5 tests and render (M4) and E17 (M8) need (from #20: M3 · Model and file I/O) | `__all__ = ["FreeTimeGaussians", "GaussiansAtTime", "covariance", "rotation_matrix"]`. `GaussiansAtTime` is a frozen dataclass with the fields `means_t`, `opacities_t`, `quats_n`, `scales`, `sh` [N, (L′+1)², 3], `opacities`, `reg_terms` and `passes_cull`: the `TemporalSlice` fields plus `sh`. `rotation_matrix(quats [..., 4]) -> [..., 3, 3]` normalizes q and applies E5's matrix in (w, x, y, z) order; `covariance(quats, scales) -> [..., 3, 3]` is R·diag(a)²·Rᵀ. Public attributes `sh_degree`, `time`, `time_unit`. `at_time(t, *, sh_degree=None)` keeps §2.1's `t: float` (normalized time) and raises `ValueError` for L′ > L or L′ < 0. `physical()` returns detached tensors under the `ftgs-if` keys with exactly the values and dtypes `to_exchange` writes (owner answer, 2026-10-12) | model sits below render and density, so both can import E5; Q-12 closed motion's `__all__` to the slice; one definition of the physical values keeps `physical()` and `to_exchange` from drifting | E5 in render or motion; `physical()` keyed by `PARAM_NAMES`; `GaussiansAtTime` limited to the five §1.1 arrays | U-5; `at_time` tests; U-22 | confirmed |

With no rows passed (N = 0, or none kept by compaction), gsplat is skipped and the render is Q-32's empty render: the background, alpha 0, zero `means2d` ([C, N, 2], or [0, 2] packed), all-False `visible` and empty `gaussian_ids`; the empty render stays on the autograd graph, with exactly zero gradients for every stored tensor and the 2D means, as the reference's does (owner answer, 2026-10-12, after the M5 milestone review's finding M-1, fixed in e689bf8). The background is composited outside the kernel in both layouts, as image + (1 − alpha)·c_bg. View matrices are built from R_w and t_w with a last row (0, 0, 0, 1), and K from fx, fy, cx and cy only; the SH camera center is −R_wᵀt_w, not gsplat's inverse of the view matrix (owner answer, 2026-10-12, during the M5 build: confirmed) | gsplat's kernels compute in float32; N = 0 kills the process with SIGFPE in both layouts, while all-culled with N > 0 works (probe); in packed mode gsplat 1.5.3's `rasterize_to_pixels` checks the background against `means2d.shape[:-2] + (3,)`, which is (3,), so a [C, 3] background fails with or without visible rows (probe); image + (1 − alpha)·c_bg equals E6's T_final·c_bg since alpha = 1 − T_final; −R_wᵀt_w is exact in real arithmetic, reads only the camera fields Q-32 names, and avoids `torch.inverse`'s host sync, one of the three §2.4 rule 3 allows; camera-major ascending order and int64 ids are what the kernel writes (probe; the docstring's int32 is wrong for 1.5.3) | a float64 model silently cast to float32; the background passed to gsplat in unpacked mode only; gsplat's `torch.inverse` for the camera center; `gaussian_ids` local to the rows passed | the CPU rejection tests (`tests/test_render.py`); empty and all-culled renders in all four combinations, per-camera backgrounds in both layouts, packed `means2d` against unpacked gathered at the ids, and the `rasterization()` cross-check (`tests/test_render_gsplat.py`) | in spec (D-76, revision 549b) |
| Q-39 | §3.1 U-7 and U-12 pass criteria; E6; Q-15 | U-7's ≤ 10⁻⁴ and U-12's criteria are undefined where both backends' float32 arithmetic can land on opposite sides of an E6 discontinuity, which changes a pixel or a row by about 1/255 or more with no bug: the α skip at 1/255 (gsplat builds with fast math and evaluates μ′ and the conic in another order), the exclusive stop at T = 10⁻⁴, the extent at the opacity threshold (gsplat's `__logf(σ_eff / float32(1/255))` against the reference's `log(255·σ_eff)`; M4 milestone review, finding 5), a radius one off where e·√Σ′ᵢᵢ is within rounding of an integer, near-equal depths, and the depth cull at near and far (from #44: M5 · gsplat renderer) | Exclusions, not a looser tolerance: pixels and rows that are kept are held to the spec's criteria. Pixels excluded: any primitive's float64 α within 10⁻³ relative of 1/255, any float64 T after a primitive within 10⁻³ relative of 10⁻⁴, or two contributing primitives whose float64 depths are within 10⁻⁵ relative. Rows excluded from the `visible` comparison: float64 e·√Σ′ᵢᵢ within 10⁻⁴ of an integer, or a box edge within 10⁻⁴ px of the image edge. Random scenes keep σ_eff at least 10⁻³ relative from 1/255 and \|z − 0.01\| ≥ 10⁻³. The excluded share is asserted ≤ 1% per scene, and the tests report the largest \|Δ\| outside the mask and the largest excluded share; U-7 also reports how many excluded pixels differ between the backends by more than its 10⁻⁴, so the record shows whether the exclusions ever change a result and whether the margins could be narrowed. First run (2026-10-12, RTX 4090, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, gsplat 1.5.3, both layouts): U-7's largest \|Δ\| outside the mask 3.4·10⁻⁶ and largest excluded share 0.23% of a camera's pixels over the 50 scenes, and none of the 128 excluded pixels differing by more than 10⁻⁴, so on this machine the exclusions changed no result; U-12's worst cosine 0.9999999 or above, worst relative L2 error 2.0·10⁻⁵ (it varies run to run in the last digit, since gsplat's backward accumulates with atomics) and largest excluded share 0.16% over the 20 scenes | redraw scenes until no pixel is near a boundary; compare without exclusions and accept seed-dependent failures | the reported excluded share and largest \|Δ\| outside the mask in U-7 and U-12, and U-7's count of excluded pixels that differ by more than 10⁻⁴ (`tests/test_render_gsplat.py`) | changed (#44) |

`packed` and `precull` → `render` (Q-36, Q-31); `adam` → `build_optimizer` (Q-44); `log_every` → Q-69's read period. `deterministic = True`: during each step `cudnn.benchmark = False`, `cudnn.deterministic = True` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True, warn_only=True)`, each restored afterwards; fused-ssim stays in use, since its backward is deterministic (source log #73); `deterministic = False` leaves the cuDNN flags as they are, and autotuning waits for B-4 (M15, §2.4 rule 2). `perc_precision`: "tf32" and "ieee" set `cudnn.allow_tf32` to True or False during each step, restored afterwards (D-52); M15 turns it on if B-4 shows the gain; (2) deterministic mode is best effort on the GPU: gsplat's backward pass still accumulates with atomic additions (§2.2), and cuBLAS calls warn unless `CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG` is set before CUDA starts, which only a process entry point can do, so it is M14's `ftgs train` or M15's to decide; (3) `prefetch` is accepted and ignored until M12, not refused like the cache placements, since it only changes speed and has a non-zero default; (4) the refusals go when M12 and M15 build those options, and the frame cache's "auto" then picks a cache instead of decoding every iteration, which changes no result (D-51 is exact)) | setting and restoring the flags around each step leaves the caller's process as it was, and `evaluate` keeps its own (D-52); | Q-78 | §3.2 S-5 ("S-2 plus 2,000 small random Gaussians"); §3.2 S-6 ("half the primitives dead … at random places away from the objects"; "except newly dead ones") | Whether S-5's added Gaussians belong to the ground truth or the initialization; how many primitives S-6 starts with; when S-6's "newly dead" check applies (from #99: M11 · Synthetic-scene recovery) | S-5: the 2,000 small random Gaussians belong to the ground truth, after S-2's 8 (committed as its own ftgs-if file): static (v = 0), always present (s = 2 in normalized time, as S-2's objects), uniform in the view volume (the cube of half-width 0.35), uniform time centers, log-uniform scales in [0.005, 0.015], uniform opacities in [0.3, 0.99], uniform colors in [0.2, 0.9]. Every row, S-2's 8 objects included, starts at its true point with opacity 0.1 (D-5, Q-79's rule, since opacity is S-5's recovered quantity); S-2's 8 objects otherwise start as in S-2, at a seeded training frame with v = 0; growth is "none" and relocation stays at its default, so N stays at 2,008 rows, one per ground-truth Gaussian, and the share compares like with like; the first criterion is asserted with three counts recorded (rows above 0.9 in each run, those whose ground truth is above 0.9, the ground truth's own 270), and the regularized run must report a nonzero L_reg at every iteration; the second criterion ("final PSNR not lower by more than 0.5 dB") stays verbatim under `xfail(strict=True)`, its cost recorded in the Calibration data table, since with growth off E8 costs about 7 dB here: on sub-pixel opaque Gaussians, Adam turns E8's small push into steady steps and σ slides down the σ·c direction (Q-81) while colors climb above the truth (second owner answer, 2026-10-13, after the M11 milestone review's F-4, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent: with growth "gradient" and N_max = 10,000, as first built, N reached 10,000 by iteration 500, about 5 copies per ground-truth Gaussian, so the share measured dilution, both runs ended below the truth's 270 rows, and the PSNR margin's sign followed N_max, +0.25 to +0.68 dB at 10,000 and −6.8 dB at 2,008; §3.2 doesn't list S-5 among the growth-off scenes, so growth "none" is this row's departure, for the next export to state). S-5 runs on the GPU only (about 21 min on the CPU, about 30 s on an RTX 4090). First changed during the M11 build (owner answer, 2026-10-13, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent) from the planning answer, which put the 2,000 in the initialization only (built by `from_init_points`): measured then, both runs ended with no σ above 0.9 (shares 0 and 0), so "lower" could not hold by construction, and the regularized run was 9 dB lower (56.2 against 47.1 dB); with σ_gt ≤ 0.9 and clutter that no image supports, nothing saturates without E8. §3.2 says "Gaussians" for scene content and "primitives" for initialization filler (S-6, S-11). Note from the two-axis review of the #99 branch: 270 of the 2,000 ground-truth Gaussians (13.5 %) have σ above 0.9, and both trained runs end with fewer such primitives than the ground truth (188 without and 114 with regularization, of 10,000), so the first criterion holds because E8 pulls true high opacities down, not because it removes spurious saturation; the owner may want this in view before the next export settles what S-5's "effect of E8" checks. S-6: N = 16, S-2's 8 live primitives plus 8 dead ones (σ = 10⁻³, scales like the ground truth's, random colors), each more than 5 × the largest ground-truth scale from every trajectory at every frame. S-6's check is made immediately after each relocation event (a callback on the step logs that carry a relocation report): no row is dead by D-14's float32-logit test, since E11's lower clamp leaves none, so "newly dead ones" is the empty set there (owner answers, 2026-10-13, while planning: S-5's placement and backend, S-6's N) | S-2's ground truth is a one-to-one scene, so clutter the training must suppress is the case E8 targets; "half the primitives" keeps S-2's full initialization; right after the event is the only point where "newly dead" is well defined | clutter in the ground truth and the initialization; N = 8 (4 live and 4 dead, leaving 4 objects uncovered in both runs); S-5 on the CPU under a new `slow` marker; checking at every iteration between events | S-5's and S-6's criteria; S-6's measured gain in the Calibration data table (`tests/synthetic/`) | in spec (D-86 and D-87, revision 549i) |
| Q-79 | §3.2 rows S-1 to S-8 (initialization, held-out views, iteration counts) and shared setup; D-5; D-66 | The rows state only some initial values; the held-out cameras and times, S-3's rig and iteration count, the image size, the loss weights and the SH degree are unstated (from #99: M11 · Synthetic-scene recovery) | The initialization is the ground truth except the quantities in the row's "What must be recovered" column, set as the row states; where the row gives no value, the package default applies (S-1's opacity starts at D-5's 0.1; its scales, rotation, time and duration are the ground truth's). S-2's "true trajectory points": each primitive at its ground-truth position at a seeded random training frame t_k, with μt = t_k and v = 0 (S-7 draws even frames only, D-66; S-8 a training image's τ). Rigs: S-1 the S-10 ring (8 cameras, radius 0.9, elevation 0.3, S = 0.99) plus 1 held-out ring camera at a new azimuth and elevation; the S-2 family and S-3 12 training hemisphere cameras at elevations 20°, 40° and 60° (four azimuths each), the radius solved so that S = 1.00 ± 0.02 (0.93), plus 4 held-out cameras at elevations 25°–55° between them, all at a focal length of 56 px; 64×64 images, the focal length chosen so every trajectory stays in view. S-3's held-out times are the training cameras at (k + ½)/30, k = 0…28; S-7 tests at odd frames 1–27 (29 lies after τ₁ = 28/30) from the 12 training cameras, and its even-frame PSNR is measured on the training images themselves; S-8's scene has no test split; S-8's per-camera offsets are uniform in [0, 1) frame. S-3 runs 10,000 iterations (changed 2026-10-13 during the M11 build with the owner's approval, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent, after the first measured run at 5,000 failed: max \|μt − μt_gt\| 0.84 frame, 11 of the 12 under 0.5, all still shrinking; durations 0.199, PSNR 51.4 dB. §3.2 shortens the calendar but not the rates, so an exponentially decayed group gets T_it/30,000 of its full-run budget: about 5 frames nominal for `times` at 5,000 (D-20), and Adam moves a primitive whose μt gradient is nonzero in a fifth of the images at about a third of its rate, so about 1.7 frames against a 2-frame shift; 10,000 gives 5× the shift nominally. A diagnostic with a constant `times` rate converged to 0.03 frame at 5,000, so nothing biases μt, and L_reg has no gradient through μt (E8). The row nearest the end of the sequence moves slowest, its footprint past the last frame. S-2 keeps 5,000. As the M11 milestone review noted, S-3's 0.5-frame criterion therefore depends on T_it, which §3.2's row doesn't fix: the next export should state S-3's count). Only S-3's count carries the owner's approval; the rest of this row still awaits confirmation, which its decision issue should say. S-3's centers are drawn over frames 3–26 (the committed ones span 3.1–24.7) so the +2-frame shift and the 3-frame duration stay inside the 30 frames, a narrower reading of "spread over 30 frames". λ_perc = 0 everywhere; S-1 trains at `sh_degree = 0`, the others at 3 (ground truth at degree 0, `shN` padded with zeros). Ground truth: S-1's Gaussian has σ ≈ 0.7, a non-gray color and scales about 0.08–0.12; S-2's are centered within 0.2 of the origin at t = ½ (0.12 in the PRD; widened while generating, before any measured run, because 8 centers 4 × 0.035 apart barely fit in a ball of 0.12–0.15 and the draw took minutes), drawn again until trajectories stay at least 4 × the largest scale apart at every frame; S-3's are static, centers over frames 3–26. These values are fixed before the first measured run, and any later change is logged in this row (owner answer, 2026-10-13, after M11: confirmed as a whole, S-3's count included; for the next spec export: S-3's iteration count, and a sentence in §3.2's shared setup that shortening scales the calendar but not the exponentially decaying rates, so a scene's count must give what it recovers a budget of several times its initial error) | the rule tests exactly what each row says must be recovered; λ_perc = 0 follows S-10, downloads nothing and gives CPU and GPU runs one configuration; setup values come from geometry, not from measured results | initialization through `from_init_points` defaults (3-NN scales collapse to the floor for one point, Q-21); S-2's initialization at the ground-truth μt; held-out cameras taken from the 12; held-out frames instead of mid-frame times; S-7 with 31 frames; λ_perc = 0.01 on the GPU under `weights`; every scene at `sh_degree = 3` | the scene criteria; the S-2 family's visibility filter in the ground-truth generator (`tests/synthetic/`) | confirmed |

| Q-82 | §3.2 S-6 ("PSNR with relocation ≥ PSNR without + 1 dB (calibrated)"); E10, E11, D-15, D-16; §1.3 "Relocation"; D-18's rationale (S-11 measures relocation under a fixed count) | The calibrated "+1 dB" is unattainable for S-2's scene under any reading: each ground-truth object is one Gaussian and S-2's initialization already sits on it, while E10 scores only live rows and relocation copies dead rows onto them (E11 splits the target's opacity and scale), never onto uncovered content. Measured at N = 16 (Q-78): with relocation 49.55 dB (CPU) and 50.09 (GPU), without 52.12 and 52.12, a gain of −2.57 and −2.04 dB (−1.89 to −2.15 dB over three GPU runs). Most of that is a confound found by the two-axis review of the #99 branch: E8 averages over all N rows, so in the run without relocation the 8 inert dead rows halve the regularizer's push on the 8 live ones (that run beats S-2's 8 rows alone by 1.9 dB); with λ_reg = 0 the gain is −0.06 dB (60.10 against 60.16 dB, CPU). What remains is relocation itself: the split pairs can't both persist (opacity is the fast variable), the copies die and are moved again, and after the window the moved rows are dead again. A Fable subagent's probe of the N = 8 reading gave +0.4 to +0.8 dB from diffuse low-opacity blobs, near gsplat's run-to-run spread of about 0.4 dB (from #99: M11 · Synthetic-scene recovery) | Keep N = 16 and §3.2's criterion verbatim under `xfail(strict=True)`, so the measurement is recorded every run and a pass would be loud; add the mechanical check that the first relocation event moves all 8 dead rows; the measured gains go to the Calibration data table (owner answer, 2026-10-13, during the M11 build, on the recommendation of a Fable subagent). For the next spec export, the owner's options: S-6's PSNR line reported rather than bounded, with "the first event moves every dead primitive" as a criterion; the fixed-N relocation comparison carried by S-11's growth = "none" row, which D-18 already names; or an S-6 ground truth with under-fitted live content where a gain can be derived | the threshold is calibrated "from analytic expectations", and the analytic expectation here is about 0 (≤ 0 at the default λ_reg, through E8's 1/N); switching to N = 8 would be tuning after a measurement and stays at noise level; an xfail keeps CI green without loosening the criterion | the N = 8 reading; the assertion left failing; a threshold set from the measurement | S-6's two tests (`tests/synthetic/`): the mechanical criteria pass, the PSNR criterion stays a strict xfail. | Q-84 | §2.1 data block and io block; U-20, U-34 to U-37, U-30; D-51, D-61 | No signatures for what U-20, U-34 to U-37 and U-30 exercise: the camera helpers, area resizing, undistortion, the YAML reader, the frame cache, the prefetcher and the scene-manifest writer (from #109: M12 · Dataset converters and frame cache) | Public additions. `openftgs.data`: `read_opencv_yaml(path) -> dict[str, Any]`; `camera_from_opencv(K, R, T, *, width, height, name="") -> Camera` (+0.5 on cx, cy); `camera_from_llff(pose, *, name="") -> Camera` (D-28); `resized_size(width, height, ratio) -> tuple[int, int]`; `resize_camera(camera, width, height) -> Camera`; `resize_area(image, width, height) -> ndarray`; `undistortion_map(K, dist, width, height) -> ndarray` [H, W, 2] float64, K in OpenCV's integer-centre convention; `undistort_image(image, K, dist) -> ndarray`; `FrameCache`: `FrameCache.default_directory(manifest) -> Path`, `FrameCache.build(dataset, *, directory=None, jobs=0) -> Path`, `FrameCache.open(dataset, *, placement, device="cpu", directory=None) -> FrameCache`, `frame(index) -> Tensor` (uint8 [H, W, 3]), the properties and attributes `nbytes`, `directory` and `placement`, and `FrameCache.size_of(dataset) -> int`; `Prefetcher(cache, *, device="cpu", depth=8)` with `schedule(indices)`, `take(index) -> Tensor` (float32 [H, W, 3]) and `close()` (the full surface listed after the two-axis review of the build). On CUDA the first iteration decodes its PNG as before; then "gpu" is chosen when `size_of` ≤ ½·free − growth (D-51: "half the GPU memory that is free after a warm-up iteration, minus what growth to N_max will add"; ½·(free − growth) in the first build, changed after the two-axis review as the more literal and more conservative reading), else "mmap", with free = `mem_get_info` free + (reserved − allocated) and growth = (n_max − N)·4·(4P + 2) bytes, P the floats per row. A cache directory that can't be created makes "auto" fall back to "off" with a `UserWarning`, on CUDA too after the first iteration; explicit placements raise `ValueError` (Q-26). The prefetcher has depth `prefetch`: on CUDA a pinned ring, a side stream and an event per buffer; on CPU a reader thread; 0 reads synchronously. `take` returns `uint8.float() / 255`, bitwise equal to `load_image`; on CUDA the divisor is a 0-dim device tensor, since there `x / 255.0` with a Python float multiplies by the reciprocal and differs from the CPU's division (measured during the build). The sampler's private `peek(k)` draws the next epoch's permutation on a clone of the generator's state; `load_state_dict` rebuilds the queue (owner answer, 2026-10-15, during the build: confirmed; note for M15: B-6's run should also record peak GPU memory at N_max with the cache on the GPU, to confirm or tighten the "auto" rule, whose growth estimate leaves the renderer's temporary buffers to the ½ margin; until then `frame_cache="mmap"` is the safe setting at the edge of GPU memory) | exact (§2.4 rule 2); conservative about memory | decide at construction; prefetch for the GPU placement too | U-30 | confirmed |

| Q-106 | §2.1 init block (`roma_init(dataset, cfg)`, `knn_velocities`, `triangulate_dlt`, `RomaInitConfig`); D-54, D-56, D-58; U-19, U-33 | `roma_init` can receive neither the devices that D-54 spreads RoMa pairs across (U-33 runs three shards on one GPU) nor its matcher, so U-19's pipeline clauses could run only with real RoMa weights on a GPU; `knn_velocities` and `triangulate_dlt` have no stated shapes, dtypes or errors; `RomaInitConfig` has no validation; no function gives the provenance D-58 records in the init file (from #133: M13 · Initialization) | `roma_init(dataset, cfg, *, devices: Sequence[str] \| None = None, matcher: Callable[[torch.device], DenseMatcher] \| None = None) -> InitPoints`. `devices` None means every visible CUDA device; each entry is one shard with its own dense matcher, so repeating an entry simulates shards on one GPU. `matcher` None means RoMa per `cfg.model`; with a matcher, `model`, `coarse_res`, `upsample_res` and `upsample_preds` are unused. `DenseMatcher` is a Protocol, `__call__(image_a, image_b) -> tuple[Tensor, Tensor]`: images [H, W, 3] float32 in [0, 1] on the CPU, as `load_image` returns them; it returns a warp [M, 4] of (x_A, y_A, x_B, y_B) in RoMa's normalized coordinates (±1 the image's outer edges) and a certainty [M] in [0, 1]. `roma_provenance(cfg) -> dict[str, str]` (Q-107). `knn_velocities(points [N, 3], next_points [M, 3], dt: float) -> [N, 3]`: v = (next[nn(i)] − pᵢ)/dt with the exact nearest neighbour, ties to the lower index, in float64 (tiled brute force on explicit coordinate differences on CUDA; SciPy's cKDTree on the CPU, candidates re-sorted by (distance, index), more neighbours queried while the last two distances are equal), returning `points`' dtype; `ValueError` for M = 0 or a dt that is zero or not finite. `triangulate_dlt(x1 [..., 2], x2 [..., 2], P1, P2) -> [..., 3]`: pixels in §1.4's continuous convention, P [3, 4] or broadcastable [..., 3, 4], the 4×4 linear system solved by SVD in float64 and dehomogenized; no filtering (which rows come out non-finite: Q-120). `RomaInitConfig.__post_init__` raises `ValueError` for a `model` outside its two values, a `coarse_res` that isn't a positive multiple of 14, `upsample_res` < 1, `matches_per_pair`, `stride` or `points_per_frame` < 1, `min_certainty` outside [0, 1], or a bool in a numeric field. `roma_init` raises `ValueError` when `devices` is None and no CUDA device is visible, and when no point survives. `__all__` = `knn_velocities`, `triangulate_dlt`, `RomaInitConfig`, `roma_init`, `roma_provenance`, `DenseMatcher` (owner answer, 2026-10-16, while planning: `devices` and `matcher`; the contracts and the validation are the plan's) | The owner's answers, as Q-16 and Q-31 did before; an injected port for the pretrained-weights dependency, with two production adapters (roma_outdoor, Tiny RoMa), as `.claude/skills/codebase-design/DEEPENING.md` §4 describes, so that U-19's pipeline clauses run on the CPU at the public interface; | Q-119 | §3.2 S-4 and the shared setup; Q-75, Q-79, Q-80 | S-4's scene, sampling, frames, schedule and where PSNR is measured are unstated (from #133: M13 · Initialization) | Three spheres translating linearly; ground-truth Gaussians on a Fibonacci lattice with long durations; 12 hemisphere training cameras plus held-out cameras; 10 frames; an `s4` seed and a committed ground-truth file. Init points: one fixed, jittered lattice per sphere, other than the ground truth's, translated to each frame with a displacement per frame below half the lattice spacing; velocities from `knn_velocities`, with a backward difference on the last frame; frame ids the frame index; durations left to `from_init_points`. Both inits train from the same `InitPoints` (velocities kept, or None) under the same budget, the shortened calendar (Q-75) and growth "none"; PSNR at the held-out cameras on the training frames; velocity error as Q-80 measures it. CPU and GPU, with a CPU target under 3 minutes, else the CPU variant becomes GPU-only as S-5 is; the measured PSNRs go in the Calibration data table. Within this, the build chose: spheres of radius 0.1, each with a step per frame drawn uniformly in 0.018–0.025 in a uniform random direction and its center at the sequence's middle time uniform within 0.2 of the origin, drawn again until its surface stays in view of all 16 hemisphere cameras (the 12 training and 4 held-out ones) and its surface at least 0.06 from the other spheres' at every frame (seed `s4.ground_truth` 1401); ground truth of 80 Gaussians per sphere on a Fibonacci lattice, sharing the sphere's velocity, with isotropic scale 0.45 × the lattice's smallest spacing, opacity 0.9, duration 10 × the sequence span (span 9/30 s), time centers at the sequence's middle time (τ = 4.5/30 s, between frames 4 and 5) and uniform random colors in [0.2, 0.9] at SH degree 0. Init points: a fixed 14-point Fibonacci lattice per sphere under a uniform random rotation, each point jittered by at most 0.05 of the lattice's smallest spacing and put back on the sphere (seed `s4.init` 1402), translated to each of the 10 frames (N = 420) and rounded to float32 before E15 runs on them; each takes the color of the nearest ground-truth Gaussian on its sphere, standing in for colors taken from the images. Training: 2,000 iterations, with relocation and λ_reg at their defaults, SH degree 3 and λ_perc = 0 (Q-79); PSNR the mean over the 4 held-out hemisphere cameras at the 10 training frames. The velocity error is Q-80's relative vector error ‖v − v_gt‖/‖v_gt‖ against the velocity of the point's sphere, in world units per τ, its median over the 420 init points asserted ≤ 5 % (S-8's single number, which bounds the relative magnitude error). S-4 runs on the CPU too, in about 76 s on a near-idle machine (settled by the build of #138, 2026-10-16) Growth "none" departs from §3.2's shared setup, which lists only S-1 to S-3, S-7 and S-8 as growth-off scenes: it is this row's departure, for the next export to state (as Q-78 states it for S-5), so that both runs keep N = 420 and "the same budget" is the same primitive count (owner answer, 2026-10-16, after the milestone review: confirmed, with the growth departure and the rationale's two additions recommended by a Fable review) | Material points persist across frames, which "displacement < half the sampling spacing" implies; the PSNR comparison follows Table 4's direction; 2,000 iterations because E15's lead over the zero-velocity init shrinks as both runs converge (scratch probes on other draws of this scene gave about 0.9–1.6 dB at 1,000–2,000 iterations and about 0.5 dB at 3,000–5,000, the CPU and the GPU within 0.01 dB), and 2,000 keeps the CPU variant under its 3-minute target The velocity half can't fail for a correct E15 (median 2.86e-7, largest 1.03e-6: float32 rounding of the positions); it carries §3.2's precondition check on the committed scene (the largest step at 0.608 of half the spacing), E15 on both k-NN paths (cKDTree and tiled CUDA) at the scene's scale, and the init points the PSNR half trains from, while E15's wiring inside `roma_init` (dt in raw units, the backward difference, slot times) is U-19's and the PSNR half carries the method's value. Resampled points are incompatible with the row, not just harder: "displacement < half the sampling spacing" is exactly the condition under which a persisting point's nearest neighbour is its own moved copy, and points drawn independently each frame on these spheres (14 to 2,000 per frame, step 0.45 × the spacing) give median relative vector errors of 200 % to 7,400 %, never near 5 % (Fable review, 2026-10-16) | points resampled independently per frame; points made by `roma_init` | S-4 | confirmed |
| Q-120 | §2.1 init block (`knn_velocities`, `triangulate_dlt`); Q-106; E15; §1.3 step 3; D-56 | Q-106 gives `knn_velocities` its errors for M = 0 and for dt, but not what malformed shapes, integer dtypes, tensors on two devices, non-finite coordinates or N = 0 do; `triangulate_dlt` has no stated errors and no output dtype, and "degenerate rows come out non-finite" doesn't say which rows are degenerate, while `torch.linalg.svd` makes a whole CPU batch raise on one NaN and returns finite vectors for non-finite rows on CUDA (source log row 129) (from #135: M13 · knn_velocities and triangulate_dlt) | `knn_velocities` also raises `ValueError` when `points` isn't [N, 3] or `next_points` isn't [M, 3], when either isn't floating point, when they sit on two devices, or when a coordinate isn't finite; N = 0 gives an empty [0, 3]. `triangulate_dlt` raises `ValueError` when `x1` or `x2` isn't [..., 2], `P1` or `P2` isn't [..., 3, 4], the batch shapes don't broadcast, an input isn't floating point, or the inputs sit on two devices. It returns float64, shape broadcast(batch shapes) + [3], on the inputs' device. The solver does no filtering: a row with a non-finite input comes out non-finite (NaN, the other rows being solved as usual), and so does a row whose null vector has an exactly zero homogeneous coordinate h₄ (rays that meet only at infinity; the division gives inf or NaN). Every other row comes out finite, a rank-deficient system with h₄ ≠ 0 included: near-degenerate rays (near-parallel, whose rounded h₄ is tiny but not 0) give a finite, far point (about 10¹⁵ away, source log row 129), which `roma_init`'s D-22 filters (depth, reprojection, angle) reject (the owner asked the advisor, Fable 5.1, for a recommendation on #135's question, 2026-10-16: no threshold inside the solver, since D-22's angle filter is the spec's rejection of near-parallel rays; this corrects Q-106's "degenerate rows come out non-finite"; confirmed by the owner, 2026-10-16, after the milestone review) | An error at the call instead of an `IndexError`, or a CUDA device-side assert, deep inside: a non-finite query point has no nearest neighbour. float64 keeps the solve's precision for `roma_init`'s filters, and Q-111 rounds positions to float32 only after them. Solving the finite rows alone keeps one bad match from aborting a batch on the CPU or passing as a plausible finite point on CUDA | PyTorch's own errors (`RuntimeError`, `IndexError`); `triangulate_dlt` returning `x1`'s dtype; a rank or σ₃ test that flags near-degenerate rows (filtering, which Q-106 rules out); NaN velocities for non-finite points | `tests/test_init.py`: the `ValueError` tests of both functions, the DLT's output dtype, the non-finite DLT rows | confirmed |

Earlier wheels (0.2.2 `torch>=2.7.1`, 0.2.4 unpinned) were compiled against older torch builds. On Python 3.11–3.13, `[init]` with `fused-local-corr>=0.2.2` therefore either moves the user's torch to the pinned release or picks a wheel whose compiled extension may not load with a newer torch (from #2: Q-n candidates: fused-local-corr torch pins (M12)) | `[init]` no longer lists fused-local-corr, on any Python: as on 3.14 (Q-6), the README tells every user to build it from its repository at commit 689f2f7 against their own torch with `pip install --no-build-isolation` (needs nvcc and a CUDA build of torch), and RoMa runs without it (`use_custom_corr` off, Q-112) until it is built. Absolute as well as relative since the end-of-build run: the 10-iteration test model's SSIM₁ is 0.0025, and its two values differed by 4.2e-9, 1.7e-6 relative (owner answer, 2026-10-18: confirmed, the rationale rewritten; the test's comment follows in #211) | the tolerance only has to separate round-off from a background mismatch, the error D-84's test is for: it sits about 240 times above the largest difference observed and orders of magnitude below the change black against white makes; it is not U-22's render tolerance carried over, since metrics amplify render differences (if every pixel moves by at most δ, PSNR moves by up to about 8.7 · δ / RMSE dB, so U-22's 1e-6 could move a 30 dB PSNR by about 3 × 10⁻⁴ dB), and the export round trip is far more accurate than U-22 requires; a tolerance derived per metric from U-22 would be loose and uneven, and SSIM and LPIPS have no simple bound | exact equality (fails on float rounding); a looser tolerance with no source in the spec | `test_eval_of_a_checkpoint_matches_the_trainers_evaluation` | confirmed |
| Q-163 | §2.1 `openftgs.io` row; D-83; Q-150 (as answered, `changed (#208)`); Q-152 | The check before training that Q-150's answer adds needs to know which `checkpoint-<n>` entries are checkpoints under Q-150, which only `io` knows, and `cli` may import only public names; the spec names no function for it (from #208: ftgs train reports a blocked checkpoint rotation as exit 2, before training (Q-150 answer)) | Public `openftgs.io.check_run_checkpoints(run_dir: Path, *, keep: int = 1) -> None`, in `__all__`: when `keep` > 1 it raises `ValueError` naming the first `RUN_DIR/checkpoint-<n>` (n in decimal, as the rotation writes it; the first by n) that isn't a checkpoint under Q-150; when `keep` = 1 it does nothing; `keep` < 1 is a `ValueError`, as for `save_run_checkpoint`. It reads only what Q-150's test reads (the manifest's format), not every file's hash, and changes nothing. `ftgs train` runs it once, before the first iteration, as an input error (exit 2, an `OSError` reading the run directory included); during the run, the rotation's own refusal (Q-150) is exit 2 like a checkpoint that can't be written, with no further save (Q-159), so an entry no save renames onto stops nothing (the build of #208, after its two-axis review, which found that running the check before every save too refused saves the rotation accepts, the final and signal saves included). During the run, every `ValueError` of `save_run_checkpoint` is exit 2, not only the rotation's refusal: `save_checkpoint`'s own checks can't fail on the trainer's state, and its refusal to replace a `RUN_DIR/checkpoint` that isn't a checkpoint is an output problem too (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed, with that sentence added) | `io` owns the checkpoint format and Q-150's rule, as for `save_run_checkpoint` (Q-152) | `cli` calls `load_checkpoint` on each entry (it refuses a kept checkpoint with a bad hash, which Q-150 counts as a checkpoint, and it verifies every hash); a check-only keyword on `save_run_checkpoint`; running the check before every save as well (refuses saves the rotation accepts) | in `tests/test_io.py`, a file `checkpoint-15` makes the check with `keep=3` a `ValueError` naming it, and passes with `keep=1`; a `checkpoint-15` with a manifest but a bad file hash passes; `checkpoint-xyz` passes; in `tests/test_cli.py`, a stray entry appearing during the run under a name no save renames onto leaves the run going to exit 0 | confirmed |

| Q-165 | §3.2 S-5 ("S-2 plus 2,000 small random Gaussians; λ_reg = 0 vs 10⁻²"), S-9; E8; D-10; D-18; Q-78; Q-81 | On S-5's scene as written, the PSNR criterion measures a property of isolated sub-pixel Gaussians rather than E8's cost on data like the paper's (Table 5 reports a gain from λ_reg = 10⁻²); M11's S-5 loses 6.8 dB under a strict xfail (Q-78, Q-81) (from #100: S-5 on a synthetic scene closer to real data) | Add an S-5 variant on S-9's ray-cast scene, with both of §3.2's criteria asserted, and keep M11's S-5 with its strict xfail; both are recorded in the Calibration data table. The parameters, fixed from analysis before any measured run: S-9's committed scene and seeds as they stand (#185's three textured spheres of radius 0.16, 16 training and 4 held-out cameras, 20 frames, 128×96), nothing chosen for S-5; S-9's surface-sample init, 300 lattice points per sphere and frame, N = 18,000, with `from_init_points`' defaults (opacity 0.1, D-5; duration Δt_f, D-21; per-frame 3-NN scales, D-25, footprints of several pixels); growth "none", so both runs keep N = 18,000 and the share compares like with like (Q-78), relocation at its default; S-9's calendar (T_it = 7,000, shortened, λ_perc = 0); λ_reg = 0 against 10⁻² (E8, D-10), the only difference; GPU only, through the `Trainer` API, the two runs shared by two tests in `tests/synthetic/test_end_to_end.py`. Measured: the share of rows with σ > 0.9 over all N (criterion 1, asserted lower with regularization); the held-out PSNR over the 4 held-out cameras × 20 frames, E16's clamp (criterion 2, asserted not lower by more than 0.5 dB); recorded beside them the sphere-coverage PSNR (Q-138), N at the end of both runs (asserted equal), and that the regularized run reports a nonzero L_reg at every iteration (asserted, as M11's S-5). Expected outcome: criterion 1 holds by a clear margin: without E8 nothing pushes the surface rows' opacities down while the images ask for full coverage, and with it the rows behind the front layer, which get almost no image gradient, drop steadily under Adam; the push per row is far weaker than on M11's scene (1/N about 9 times smaller, and D-21's one-frame duration gates it to roughly an eighth of the iterations), but Adam normalizes its size. Criterion 2 holds, \|ΔPSNR\| ≤ 0.5 dB with its sign uncertain: inside a dense surface what lies behind a front primitive is more textured surface, not black, so Q-81's σ·c degeneracy survives only at silhouettes against black, a small share of the pixels (the spheres cover about 16 % of the held-out pixels); S-9's run-to-run spread is about 0.1 dB, well under the margin. If a criterion fails on the one measured run, its assertion goes under `xfail(strict=True)` with the values in the Calibration data table, the scene unchanged (Q-78's precedent), for the owner to decide through #102. #100's brief planned this inside M14; M14 closed without it, and the ray caster it waited for exists, so it runs on its own (owner's request, 2026-10-19) (owner answer, 2026-10-19: confirmed before the measured run). The scene departs from §3.2's S-5 text, for the next spec export to state: S-5 also runs on S-9's ray-cast scene, both criteria asserted, while M11's scene and its PSNR line wait on #102 (added after the two-axis review of #100) | the comparison between the two runs needs no Gaussian ground truth; S-9's scene is the suite's closest to real data, and reusing it unchanged leaves nothing tuned for S-5; the same N in both runs keeps the share from measuring dilution (Q-78) | replace M11's S-5 outright; record the PSNR line rather than bound it; λ_reg = 0 against 10⁻² on openftgs-synth's `kitchen` in M17 (D-79), after M13's initialization, as fallback evidence if this scene leaves the question open | `test_s5_raycast_regularization_keeps_fewer_primitives_opaque` and `test_s5_raycast_regularization_costs_at_most_half_a_db` (`tests/synthetic/test_end_to_end.py`, GPU), and their values in the Calibration data table | in spec (D-86, revision 549i) |

| Q-180 | §2.1 `ftgs bench` and the exit codes; §3.4 ("JSON with the GPU model, driver, CUDA, PyTorch and gsplat versions"); Q-166; Q-130 | Q-166 fixes BENCH.json's top-level layout and the exit codes, but leaves open what `command`, `case`, `figure` and `kind` hold; the scene's `cameras`, `frames`, `width` and `height`; where the driver's version comes from, PyTorch having no public accessor; what "the commit with a dirty flag" is for an installed package; the order of the checks; and what a benchmark whose ticket hasn't landed does without CUDA (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | `command` is the command line as a list, `["ftgs", "bench", SUBCOMMAND, SCENE, "--out", OUT]` and `--model MODEL` when given, the paths as typed. A case is `{"case": {parameter: value}, "figures": {name: value}}` (B-3: `{"impl", "n"}`, and `{"impl", "resizes"}` for the recompilations), times in seconds (names ending `_s`) and memory in bytes. A budget entry names its `case` too, beside Q-166's keys; its `figure` is a key of that case's figures, and its `kind` is `budget` (a §3.4 budget), `bound` (an upper bound standing for one, as Q-168's for B-1) or `limit` (another spec limit checked on the run, as U-28's 2 recompilations in B-3). The scene's `path` is absolute, `sha256` that of the manifest's bytes, `cameras` the cameras with images, `frames` the most images any one camera has, `width` and `height` the first training image's; `date` is the start of the run in UTC, ISO 8601 to the second. The environment block holds `gpu` (`name` of the current device and `count`; null and 0 without CUDA), `driver` (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=driver_version`, then `kmd_version`; null when nvidia-smi can't answer; source log 162), `cuda` (`torch.version.cuda`), `cudnn` (`torch.backends.cudnn.version()`), `torch`, `gsplat`, `fused_ssim` (null when absent) and `python`, and also `openftgs` and `cpu` (the CPU's model, for figures bound by the host); `commit` and `dirty` come from git in the folder of the imported package, only when its `__init__.py` is a tracked file there (not for a wheel inside a checkout's `.venv`), `dirty` meaning that tracked files differ from the commit, both null otherwise. The checks run in this order: `--model` on a subcommand that doesn't take it (a usage error, exit 2), the scene (exit 2), CUDA except for `convert` (exit 2), MODEL (exit 2); then a benchmark not yet built keeps Q-130's message and exit 1, writing nothing, and a built one makes BENCH.json's folder (exit 2 if it can't, before running), runs, and replaces the file atomically (Q-126) | everything a later comparison needs is in the file, keyed for Q-170's comparison by benchmark, case and figure; the dirty flag guards against numbers from uncommitted code; a missing CUDA is an environment error whether the benchmark is built yet or not | `command` as one string; cases as strings such as "compiled/n=100000"; `frames` as the distinct training timestamps, which counts every image of an unsynchronized rig; pynvml for the driver; untracked files counting as dirty | `tests/test_bench.py`: the layout and environment test and the exit-code tests; `tests/test_cli.py`: the test that replaces Q-130's | provisional |
| Q-181 | §3.4 setups; Q-168 ("rows on a background sphere and 32 random spheres around the scene's centre, scales from the rows' spacing with the normal axis at 1/5, σ ~ U[0.3, 0.95], SH degree 3, durations set for the target active fraction, seeded"); D-27 | Q-168 leaves open what "the scene's centre" is, the spheres' sizes and places, the background sphere's radius and share of the rows, the factor between spacing and scale, the motion, the colours, the times and durations that give "the target active fraction", and the order of the rows. D-27's centroid of the training camera centres is no centre of the content on a forward-facing rig: on kitchen it lies about 2.6 units behind the cameras' convergence point (S = 1.60), and spheres around it would leave 92 to 98 % of the rows outside each camera's image (source log 163) (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | The centre is the point nearest every training camera's optical axis, by least squares, with a ridge of 10⁻⁶ × the trace of the normal matrix toward the point S ahead of the camera centres' centroid along the cameras' mean axis, which matters only where the axes are parallel. The view radius ρ is the smallest, over the training cameras, of the camera's distance to the centre times the tangent of its narrower half field of view. ⌊N/4⌋ rows lie on a static background sphere about the centre, of radius twice the largest camera distance, so it encloses every camera; the others on 32 spheres, their centres uniform in the ball of radius ρ/2 about the centre and their radii U[0.05, 0.15]·ρ, each row drawn to a sphere with probability ∝ its area. Each sphere moves at its own velocity, a uniform direction at a speed U[0, 0.25]·ρ per unit of normalized time, its centre at its drawn place at t = ½; each row sits at a uniform direction on its sphere as the sphere is at the row's μt, so E1 keeps it on the moving sphere. Tangent scales are the spacing √(4πr²/n) of the n rows on a sphere of radius r, the normal axis 1/5 of it, along the sphere's normal; σ ~ U[0.3, 0.95]. For an active fraction f < 1, μt ~ U[−r, 1 + r] with r = f/(2(1 − f)), and s = r/√(2 ln 255σ): a row passes the cull (D-32) while \|t − μt\| ≤ r, so at any t in [0, 1] a share f of the rows does, in expectation; for f = 1, μt ~ U[0, 1] and s = 10. Colours are per sphere U[0.2, 0.8] per channel (per row U[0.3, 0.7] on the background), plus N(0, 0.05²) per row, clamped to [0, 1], as sh0; shN ~ N(0, 0.02²). Everything is drawn in float64 from one generator on the model's device, seeded, then the rows are shuffled and rounded to float32, so a prefix is a smaller model of the same kind. A scene with a single timestamp is exit 2 (D-71 leaves no row inactive) | the content must be in view of every camera, on ring and forward-facing rigs alike, for B-1, B-2 and B-7 to time what a camera renders: the convergence point is a ring rig's centre and a forward-facing rig's look-at point, and the view radius sizes the content by what the cameras see, where S measures the rig's spread; activity uniform over [0, 1] matches training, which samples every frame; shuffled rows make B-3's growing prefixes resizes as at growth events | D-27's centroid as the centre, as the ticket's pointer reads; sizes from S; an active fraction exact at one probe time only; a background plane facing the cameras | the private tests `test__synthesize_*` in `tests/test_bench.py`: N, seeding and σ's range; the active fraction within 0.01 at five times for 5 %, 20 % and 100 %; at least 70 % of the rows in front of and inside the image of every camera of a ring rig and of a forward-facing one (78 % measured on both, and on kitchen; 2 to 8 % around the centroid instead) | provisional |
| Q-182 | §3.4 B-3 ("N from 10⁵ to 3·10⁶, with resizes as at growth events"; "sets D-50's choice; recompilations counted"); D-50; Q-13; Q-168; U-28 | The sizes between 10⁵ and 3·10⁶, the model, the times, the outputs the backward starts from, the warm-up, how the first call is timed, how the sizes are resized, and what B-3 decides, B-1 deciding the CUDA operator (PRD #220's rule) (from #225: M15 · ftgs bench framework and B-3) | 11 sizes: 10⁵, 1.5·10⁵, 2·10⁵, 3·10⁵, 4·10⁵, 5·10⁵, 7·10⁵, 10⁶, 1.5·10⁶, 2·10⁶ and 3·10⁶, B-1's 0.5M, 1M and 3M among them, so 10 resizes. Each size takes the first n rows of one model of 3·10⁶ rows synthesized as Q-181 says (seed 0, 20 % active, on the GPU), so each resize keeps the rows before it in order and appends new ones; the seven tensors `temporal_slice` takes become fresh leaves at each size. Every placement is tried on the scene given. `gpu` runs only where "auto" would choose it: a probe trainer of the case's configuration with `frame_cache` "off", the path "auto" takes on its first CUDA iteration, runs one iteration, and then free (`mem_get_info`'s free + reserved − allocated), growth ((n_max − N)·4·(4P + 2), P the floats of a row) and the cache's `size_of` are read as the trainer's rule reads them, but once `step()` has returned, where the rule reads them inside it while the iteration's render output, loss and image are still held, so the probe's free memory is larger by those tensors, under 1 % of it; a cache above ½·free − growth is skipped, listed in `setup.skipped` with its reason and that estimate, with no case or budget (kitchen's on a 24 GiB GPU); otherwise the case runs with `frame_cache` "gpu", and its figures add the estimate (`auto_free_bytes`, `auto_growth_bytes`, `auto_allocated_bytes`, `auto_reserved_bytes`, `cache_bytes`, `auto_limit_bytes`), the device's total (`auto_total_bytes`) and what lies outside this process's allocator (`auto_outside_bytes` = total − free − allocated: other processes and the CUDA context, which `mem_get_info`'s free memory leaves out; on this machine the desktop session alone holds about 380 to 560 MiB that can't be freed, coordinator's note during #232), and Q-99's margin: `beyond_estimate_bytes` = peak reserved − the probe's allocated − the cache − growth, what the run took beyond the rule's estimate, and `margin_used` = that over ½·free, the share of the rule's margin it used; at most 1 means a cache at the rule's limit would still have fit, judged from reserved memory, so conservatively. Budget entries (`kind: "budget"`, limit 0.01, figure `data_share`, Q-180) for `gpu` and `mmap` at both depths at 1M rows; none for `off`, which reads no cache, or for the 3M case | B-1's warm-up and ratio of means make B-6's `mmap` figure comparable with B-1's `data` share; leaving events out is conservative; "auto"'s own rule, measured where "auto" measures it, decides whether the GPU cache fits, while an explicit `gpu` placement of kitchen's cache would fill the GPU and end the run out of memory; the probe uses the trainer's public interface, not its private state; prefetch 0 is a valid setting and #222 asks whether its wait matters, so it is held to the same 1 %; the wall window says where a host wait goes when the CUDA events can't see it | timing the 200 iterations with their events; a fixed rule, such as the cache against half the GPU's total memory; running the `gpu` case under "auto" and reading the trainer's private choice; choosing the placements by scene, as Q-178 words it; a budget for `mmap` at depth 8 only; no host window | `test__data_config_is_b1s_with_the_case_placement`, `test__auto_estimate_applies_q99s_rule_and_measures_its_margin`, `test__data_figures_share_the_data_wait_over_iterations_without_an_event`, `test__data_budgets_hold_the_cache_placements_under_1_percent`, `test_b6_shares_the_data_wait_per_placement` and `test_b6_skips_the_gpu_placement_where_auto_would_not_choose_it` (`tests/test_bench.py`); B-6's Calibration rows | provisional |
| Q-T228.1 | §3.4 B-4 ("LPIPS-VGG in IEEE float32, TF32 and bfloat16, channels-last on and off; SSIM fused and in PyTorch"; "1352×1014 and 1920×1080"; "sets D-52's defaults"); D-52; §2.4 rule 2; Q-168; Q-173; Q-180 | B-4's setup is unstated beyond its two sizes: the images, what "channels-last on and off" covers, what one call times, the repetitions, the order of the cases, how the PyTorch SSIM path is reached, how bf16's deviation is measured, and which pairs of cases the gains of Q-173's rule compare (from #228: M15 · B-4 (bench losses), bf16 and D-52's exact options) | **Images**: the target is the scene's first training image, resized bilinearly with antialiasing to each size when its own differs (on kitchen, 1920×1080 stretches 4:3 to 16:9); the prediction is that image plus Gaussian noise of σ 0.05 from a CPU generator seeded with 0; both [H, W, 3] float32 on CUDA. **LPIPS**, lpips 0.1.4's VGG network called directly (frozen, in evaluation mode), its inputs mapped to [−1, 1] by lpips's `normalize` (D-68's mapping), in three layouts: `nchw` (contiguous NCHW inputs and weights); `channels_last_inputs` (channels-last inputs, NCHW weights), which is what `LPIPSLoss` gives the network in training, since its batch-first view of a [..., H, W, 3] render is channels-last and every convolution then runs channels-last; `channels_last_inputs_and_weights` (a copy of the network converted with `to(memory_format=torch.channels_last)`). Three precisions: `ieee` (`cudnn.allow_tf32` False), `tf32` (True), `bf16` (bfloat16 autocast around the network, TF32 on as Q-172 has it, the value cast to float32). cuDNN autotuning off and on; cuDNN's deterministic algorithms off and float32 matrix products at "highest" in every case (D-52), restored afterwards. One call is the forward on both images and the mean (stage `loss.perceptual`), then `torch.autograd.grad` of that value to the prediction (stage `backward`); the target never requires a gradient. **SSIM**: `losses.ssim` with `LossConfig`'s window, σ and padding (11, 1.5, "valid"), its forward (stage `loss.ssim`) and the prediction's gradient: fused-ssim in the benchmark's own process when `fused_ssim` imports there, and the PyTorch path in the child processes below (`python -c`, which sets `sys.modules["fused_ssim"] = None` before anything imports it, so `import fused_ssim` raises `ImportError` and `ssim` takes its fallback; a child raises if it can still import fused-ssim, and it gets the folder the benchmark imported openftgs from first on its PYTHONPATH), with TF32 on as in training (D-52) and autotuning off and on. **Measurement** as Q-168's: the first call on the wall clock, 10 warm-up calls, then the medians over 30 calls of the forward, the backward and the span from the forward's start to the backward's end, from the stage clock's CUDA events; the peak allocated memory per case. **Processes**: cuDNN caches the algorithm it picked for a configuration and looks there first whatever `cudnn.benchmark` says, so the two autotuning settings can't be compared in one process, in either order; the deviations (autotuning off) and fused-ssim run in the benchmark's process, and every LPIPS case and the PyTorch SSIM path in one child process per autotuning setting, off and then on, started after the benchmark has released its cached GPU memory. **Deviation**, per size, with the network as training runs it (channels-last inputs, NCHW weights, autotuning off): the value and the prediction's gradient under `tf32` and `bf16` against `ieee`, as the two values and \|v − v_ieee\| / \|v_ieee\|, ‖g − g_ieee‖₂ / ‖g_ieee‖₂, and max\|g − g_ieee\| / max\|g_ieee\|, in float64. **Gains**: 1 − variant / baseline of the median forward plus backward, per size, then the smallest over the sizes and whether it reaches Q-173's 3 %: `autotuning` (TF32, channels-last inputs, off → on) and `channels_last_weights` (TF32, autotuning off, channels-last inputs → inputs and weights), which the rule decides; reported beside them, both together, `tf32` (IEEE → TF32), `bf16` (TF32 → bf16) and `fused_ssim` (the PyTorch path without autotuning → fused-ssim). The rule's other half, B-1 no worse, is a rerun of B-1 with what B-4 turned on. **Cases** (Q-180): `{"loss": "lpips_vgg", "width", "height", "precision", "layout", "autotuning"}`, `{"loss": "ssim", "width", "height", "path": "fused"}`, `{"loss": "ssim", "width", "height", "path": "torch", "precision": "tf32", "autotuning"}`, `{"deviation", "width", "height"}`, `{"comparison", "width", "height"}` and `{"comparison"}`; no budget entry, since B-4 has none (§3.4) | measurable in minutes on one GPU; the network and its inputs as training has them, so the gains are training's; natural image content gives the deviation realistic feature statistics, and a fixed seed keeps it repeatable; a child process keeps `ssim()`'s signature (PRD #220, owner's decision); a process per setting keeps cuDNN's algorithm cache from carrying one setting's picks into the other | random images; the prediction as another frame of the same camera; both autotuning settings in one process, in either order; the NCHW cases through `LPIPSLoss`; gains from means; gains against the faster autotuning setting | `test_b4_times_every_lpips_variant_and_both_ssim_paths` and `test_b4_records_bf16s_deviation_and_the_gains_of_its_comparisons` (`tests/test_bench.py`); the B-4 Calibration rows | provisional |
| Q-T228.2 | D-52 ("bfloat16 LPIPS (`perc_precision = "bf16"`) is an approximate option"); `PerfConfig.perc_precision`; Q-172 | Where bf16 applies when the trainer runs on the CPU (the reference backend), and what precision the network's final reductions keep (from #228: M15 · B-4 (bench losses), bf16 and D-52's exact options) | The trainer's private `LPIPSLoss` subclass runs the network under `torch.autocast` on the images' device type in bfloat16, so a CPU run is in bfloat16 too, PyTorch's CPU autocast covering the convolutions; the network's own averages and the mean over the batch run as autocast leaves them, and only the result is cast to float32, as the owner's decision says ("returns float32") | one rule on every device, so a CPU test pins the option; the option is opt-in and approximate, and B-4 records its deviation on the GPU | bf16 only on CUDA, float32 on the CPU; refusing bf16 on the CPU; casting each layer's average to float32 before their sum | `test_a_bf16_step_gives_a_finite_perceptual_term_near_float32` on the CPU and the GPU (`tests/test_train.py`) | provisional |
